| 1.26 | `gradio.md`: file-access controls (allowed_paths/blocked_paths/cache) | Done, #121. The guide had auth and TLS but nothing on file exposure. Added section 4 "Restrict file access": `launch()` serves files over `/gradio_api/file=`; the default set is `set_static_paths` files, `allowed_paths`, and the cache, and a path the app returns from a function is also served if it lies in `allowed_paths`, the working directory, or the temp directory (the cache is shared across all users). Guidance favours an allowlist over a blocklist: do not launch from a directory with `.env`/keys/private source; scope `allowed_paths`/`set_static_paths` narrowly, as absolute paths (a directory exposes all its files); treat `blocked_paths` as a fragile backstop (it takes precedence, but filesystem-quirk bypasses of path blocklists have occurred) and keep Gradio current; never return user input to a file-based output component (a returned path in the working or temp directory is served). Added a Verify check (allowed marker returns, blocked marker refused) and the file-access guide to Sources; renumbered Verify to section 5. Verified at gradio.app/guides/file-access. (M, S), 2026-09-13 audit. |

All map with no host IP (0.0.0.0). The guide framed `serve` as "authenticated by membership" and "device identity and your tailnet ACLs" without saying the default policy is allow-all (`src` `*`, `dst` `*:*`; empty `acls` = allow-all, not deny-all), so every device and user in the tailnet reaches the service until it is scoped with a grant (or ACL) from a specific user, group, or tag to this host and port. Added that caveat plus the point that tailnet access is network reachability, not user authorization (a panel behind `serve` still needs its own login). Verified at tailscale.com access-control docs; source added. (H, S), 2026-09-13 audit. |
| 1.6 | `docker.md`: DOCKER-USER chain and the Docker 28.0 publishing caveat | Done, #116. The guide gave only loopback publishing. Added: the host-IP restriction in a publish string is reliable only on Docker Engine 28.0+, because before 28.0 a neighbour host could reach a loopback-mapped port, a remote host could reach a port bound to a specific host IP, and an unpublished container port was reachable by direct routing (moby #49325, #48724); and the `DOCKER-USER` chain (processed before Docker's own accept rules) for restricting a routable-interface publish by source, with the post-DNAT caveat that its rules match a container's internal address, not the published port. Re-verify note: the row's premise that DOCKER-USER is documented on the cited packet-filtering page no longer holds (Docker split its network docs); cited the current "Docker with iptables" page and the 28.0 release notes instead. Verified at docs.docker.com. The DOCKER-USER restriction carries an inline confirm (`iptables -nvL` plus an external probe); its live demonstration ships reasoned, tracked as row 1.72. 3 families flagged; 2026-09-13 audit. |
| 1.32 | `admin-uis.md`: Adminer section | Done, #115. The single-file PHP database manager scanners probe at `adminer.php` was missing from the panel list. Added a section: Adminer keeps no account store of its own (its login form takes the database server's own address, username, and password), so an internet-reachable Adminer is a public login form onto the database and a perennial exploit target; it does not belong on a public vhost, serve it only behind proxy-level TLS and authentication with source-IP restriction, and delete the file from the docroot when unused. Verified at adminer.org (database management in a single PHP file; connects to a database server with username and password); source added. 2026-09-13 audit. |
| 1.33 | `secrets.md`: credential-bearing config files (tfstate, kubeconfig, docker config) | Done, #114. Rule 1 named `.env`/`*.key`/`*.pem`; it did not name the working files that embed live credentials in plaintext. Added rule 9: a Terraform state file (`terraform.tfstate` and its `.backup`) stores every resource attribute including secrets and local state is unencrypted; a kubeconfig carries a base64 client private key in `client-key-data` (and may hold a token); `~/.docker/config.json` stores registry logins base64-encoded, not encrypted, without a credential helper. Gitignore them beside `.env`/`*.pem`; the rule-4 scanners catch one committed anyway. Three facts verified at the Terraform (manage-sensitive-data), Kubernetes (kubeconfig), and Docker (login) docs, all added to Sources. 2026-09-13 audit. |
| 1.28 | `ollama.md`: unfiltered ss in the Verify step | Done, #113. The Verify piped `ss -tlnp` through `grep 11434`, which apache.md:88 and traefik.md:144 document against: a port filter hides an unexpected listener and matches the port's digits inside an address (an IPv6 literal, say). Now runs bare `ss -tlnp`, so the reader reads every listener and confirms 11434 on 127.0.0.1 with nothing unexpected beside it. 2026-09-13 audit; ~18 other guides share the `ss | grep` pattern, filed for a convention decision (open-findings/pending-decisions 2026-09-15). |
| 1.29 | `memcached.md`: unfiltered ss in the Verify step | Done, #113. Same fix as 1.28: `ss -tlnup | grep 11211` became bare `ss -tlnup`, which also confirms no UDP line (memcached's UDP amplifier surface) while showing the whole listener table on the intended address. |
| 3.12 | `tools/check_csp_hashes.py`: cover site/404.html (multi-page CSP gate) | Done, #112. The gate hard-coded `PAGE=site/index.html` and modeled exactly one `<style>` and one `<script>`, so `site/404.html` (one style, no script; its style hash pinned in the shared `/*` CSP since #102) was never read, and editing it left the gate green while a browser refused the style. Generalized to a declared per-page list `PAGES` (index style:1/script:1, 404 style:1/script:0); refuses any `site/**/*.html` not listed (closes the unlisted-page fail-open); tightened the `_headers` scan to require exactly one CSP under `/*` and forbid any other rule setting or detaching CSP (a page served at any request path can be governed by a path-scoped rule); model refusals accumulate per page; both CSP kinds resolved unconditionally; membership iterates observed blocks with a verified-count cross-check. Harness generalized: writes both pages, 43 ported cases plus 25 new (edited-404-style FAILs, a block added to the script-free page FAILs, an unlisted page FAILs, happy path proves 3 blocks across 2 pages); 68 cases total. Tier Sensitive: tri-family design seeds combined by Fable, drafter re-verified premises at source, adversarial self-checks confirmed the fail-open closed and that the harness catches a single-page regression. Orphan-pin exactness deferred to 3.15. |
| 4.11 | `site/index.html`: homepage copy-paste prompt for confidential business rules | Done, #111. secrets.md rule 8 (shipped #108) treats a confidential pricing formula, allocation rule, or customer-specific process as a secret that belongs out of public repositories, client bundles, and public LLM prompts, but the homepage copy-paste prompts had no task that surfaced them. Added an "Audit for exposed business rules" prompt card matching the five existing ones: it directs an assistant to secrets.md and web-exposure.md, has it search the public repo and git history, the client bundle (NEXT_PUBLIC_/VITE_ values, client-shipped code, source maps), and public-LLM prompts, report exposures as a table, and apply the per-guide fix only after explicit approval. No new inline script or style, so no CSP hash change; the copy button targets an existing `<pre id>`. Second external review (item 6, homepage half; the secrets.md/README bullet was the #108 half); maintainer approved 2026-09-15 (Small hook). Completes the second external review. |
| 4.10 | README.md + CONTRIBUTING.md: name the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications | Done, #110. Both scope statements said general application security (injection, deserialization, business logic) is out of scope and pointed to OWASP resources, but neither named the LLM-application-layer list. Each now also points to the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications (https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/, the 2025 edition, LLM01 through LLM10:2025) for LLM-specific risks such as prompt injection. No new guide; the repo's own scope stays deployment exposure. The condensed homepage scope teaser (site/index.html) was left unchanged by choice, since it already omits the injection/deserialization enumeration. Second external review (item 5); maintainer approved 2026-09-15 |
| 1.70 | `vector-databases.md` + `chat-uis.md`: cross-user isolation Verify | Done, #109. The guides configured tenant RLS (pgvector) and per-workspace access (AnythingLLM) but verified only network exposure and login, not data isolation. vector-databases.md adds a cross-tenant probe: connect as tenant B, run tenant A's similarity search, confirm only B's rows return; an RLS-bypassing role (superuser or owner) returns both, so a difference is the policy filtering not an empty table; a query returning another tenant's vectors is a live cross-user leak. chat-uis.md adds a RAG/chat leak check: as user B, ask a question answerable only from user A's documents or chats and confirm nothing of A's returns; the fix is a per-user or per-workspace retrieval filter, cross-referencing the vector-databases RLS check. Instantiates controls-reference sec4/sec7 for the chat/RAG context. Second external review (item 3); maintainer approved 2026-09-15 |
| 1.69 | `secrets.md`: keys/customer data and business rules out of LLM prompts | Done, #108. Added rules 7 and 8. Rule 7: keys, credentials, and customer data stay out of AI-model prompts, including the system prompt and any internal role map placed there, since a system prompt is not a secret store and prompt injection can make a model repeat it; an assistant that needs a credential gets a scoped, revocable key through the tool's config, not inline. Rule 8: unpublished business rules (pricing, allocation, a customer-specific process) are intellectual property treated like secrets, kept out of public repos, client bundles (NEXT_PUBLIC_/VITE_), and public LLMs. Complements the existing reactive chat/log/paste-is-burned line. Second external review; maintainer approved 2026-09-15 (items 2, 4, and the item-6 secrets bullet) |
| 1.68 | `headers.md`: browser-side cache for authenticated pages | Done, #107. The guide covered only the shared-cache A-to-B case; it now covers the browser's own cache. `private` lets the browser keep a page so the Back button can redisplay an authenticated view after logout; `no-store` is required on authenticated or session-cookie responses; and logout should send `Clear-Site-Data` (its `cache` directive clears the browser and back/forward cache, `cookies` drops the session). The Verify adds a back-button-after-logout check. Verified at MDN Cache-Control, Clear-Site-Data, and bfcache. Second external review (private/plans/friend-review-2-2026-09-15.md) |
| 1.44 | eleven legacy probe guards to the rule-6 template | Done, #106. agent-builders, chat-uis, cloud-firewalls, cloud-identity-proxies, deployment-lifecycle (two guards, IPv4 and IPv6), devops-uis, fronting-auth, host, image-gen-uis, mcp-servers, and ollama each carried the pre-hardening guard: an unquoted placeholder on the `set --` line, no `PASTE_WHOLE_BLOCK` sentinel or arity check, and no `-g` on the probe. Each now uses the full rule-6 template: `set -- PASTE_WHOLE_BLOCK 'REPLACE_WITH_...'` with the substitute-inside-the-quotes note, the sentinel-and-shift check, `[ "$#" -eq 1 ]`, `case "$1"`, and `-g` on every probe curl (nc/nmap/docker probes take the header only). shellcheck and the new guard-conventions gate (#105) verify them; the gate's enablement on main stays clean. ai-infra-services.md and CONTRIBUTING.md already carried the idiom. External review, guard-conventions lineage |
| 3.11 | gate the two guard conventions (curl `-q` leading, glob URL carries `-g`) | Done, #105. `tools/check_guard_conventions.py` is a stdlib shell-lexer gate, not line-regex, so flag clusters, `--disable`/`--globoff` long forms, comments, heredoc data, line-continuations, and command wrappers are handled: C1-MISSING-Q and C1-LATE-Q flag a fenced-block curl whose first argument is not `-q`/`--disable` (curl only skips `~/.curlrc` when `-q` leads, so a late `-q` is inert), and C1-MISSING-G flags an unglobbed `[...]` or `{...}` in a scheme-bearing URL without `-g`. Registered with `--no-c2` and `--min-curls 176`. A 60-case self-test guards the gate; enablement on main is clean (183 fences, 181 curls, 0 findings). C2 (probe-outside-guard) is deferred to row 3.14 because the corpus's legitimate exit-guard-then-probe-after-esac idiom trips the sentinel rule. #67 was held after the plan's blanket-`-g` and probe-inside-guard premises were falsified against the live corpus (F-GUARD-GATE-PLAN-FALSIFIED); the committed program already carried the narrow `-g`, the maintainer chose the NARROW-GATE (2026-09-14) and confirmed build-gate-first (2026-09-15). SENSITIVE tier, independent adversarial QA. |
| 1.67 | `devops-uis.md`: add leading `-q` to the TLS positive-control probe | Done, #104. The Docker-over-TLS Verify block ran `env -u ... curl -sS -o /dev/null ... --cacert ca.pem https://.../_ping` without a leading `-q`, so a `~/.curlrc` (proxy, insecure, output redirect) could silently alter the probe; the next curl in the same block already led with `-q`. Prepended `-q`. Surviving genuine defect from the guard-conventions-gate review (F-MISSING-Q); the two it originally named, fronting-auth.md and image-gen-uis.md, were fixed in later PRs. Verified no other corpus curl lacks a leading -q/--disable. External review, row 3.11 lineage |
| 3.13 | sync `plugin.json` version to VERSION | Done, #103. `plugin/plugin.json` was frozen at 1.0.38 since the plugin was packaged (#39) while the repo reached 1.0.102, so an adopter's updater keyed on the plugin version had no signal to re-fetch corrected guides, and the version misrepresented what the bundle contained. `scripts/build-plugin.sh` now rewrites the manifest version from VERSION on every build (idempotent sed on the version field); the existing plugin-staleness gate rebuilds the bundle and diffs, so a drifted plugin.json version is caught with no new gate. plugin.json is now 1.0.103. Maintainer chose sync-plus-build-step over an independent plugin version. External pre-launch review; maintainer approved 2026-09-15 |
| 4.9 | add a `site/404.html` page | Done, #102. Unknown URLs were served the homepage with HTTP 200; Cloudflare Pages serves 404.html for an unmatched path when the file exists. Added a self-contained, theme-aware 404 matching the site (padlock brand, accent, links to the site index and repo), with one inline stylesheet whose sha256 is pinned in site/_headers so the CSP (default-src none, hashed style-src) allows it. check_csp_hashes.py still pins only index.html; covering 404.html needs the gate generalized past its one-style-one-script model, tracked as row 3.12. Verified the pinned hash with the gate's own hashing logic. External pre-launch review; maintainer approved shipping 2026-09-15 |
| 4.8 | add a `SECURITY.md` security policy | Done, #101. The repository shipped without one. SECURITY.md routes reports to GitHub private vulnerability reporting, defines scope (guides, the plugin, and helper scripts, with documentation accuracy explicitly in scope), and notes that main is the published state versioned as 1.0.<PR#> with no separate release artifact. Added to not_a_guide() in run_all_checks.sh and NOT_A_GUIDE in check_guide_shape.py so a meta file is not held to guide shape. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md); maintainer approved shipping 2026-09-15 |
| 1.66 | `ray.md` + `mlflow.md`: guard the external-reachability Verify probes (rule 6) | Done, #100. Each guide's "is the port reachable from outside" probe used `http://REPLACE_WITH_THE_SERVER_PUBLIC_IP:PORT/` with a pass of `http=000` and `time_connect=0.000000`; an unsubstituted placeholder fails DNS to exactly that, a false pass indistinguishable from a genuinely filtered port (CONTRIBUTING rule 6). Both probes now use the corpus's guarded subshell idiom (`set --` then `case *REPLACE_WITH_*|""` refuses to run unsubstituted, `--noproxy '*'` so a proxy cannot answer, and the `exitcode`/`errormsg` write-out naming why the connection failed), the same pattern already in chat-uis.md/devops-uis.md/fronting-auth.md; the curl Sources bullets now cite the exitcode/errormsg variables (curl 7.75.0). Verified with shellcheck 0.11.0. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.65 | `model-servers.md`: correct vLLM's default bind to 0.0.0.0 | Done, #99. The intro listed only TGI and Triton as 0.0.0.0 binders, implying vLLM defaults to local; but `vllm serve` leaves `--host` unset (FrontendArgs.host defaults to None) and the launcher binds `(args.host or "", port)`, i.e. all interfaces, logging `http://0.0.0.0:8000`. The guide now lists vLLM among the all-interfaces binders and adds `--host 127.0.0.1` to keep it on loopback, with the launcher source. Verified at vllm/entrypoints/launchers/cli_args.py and launcher.py (2026-09-14). External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.64 | `ollama.md`: note the Ollama Docker image's all-interfaces default | Done, #98. The guide said Ollama binds 127.0.0.1 by default, which is true for the standalone binary, but the official ollama/ollama Docker image sets ENV OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0:11434, so inside a container Ollama listens on every interface and a bare `-p 11434:11434` republishes it to every host interface without the reader setting anything. The guide now explains that in Docker the control is the published address (`-p 127.0.0.1:11434:11434`), not OLLAMA_HOST, because the container must listen broadly for a same-network proxy to reach it, and adds the Dockerfile source. Verified at the ollama/ollama Dockerfile (2026-09-14). External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.63 | `elasticsearch.md`: warn about the OpenSearch demo internal_users.yml accounts | Done, #97. The demo security configuration seeds seven built-in accounts (`admin`, `anomalyadmin`, `kibanaserver`, `kibanaro`, `logstash`, `readall`, `snapshotrestore`); the guide already told readers to set OPENSEARCH_INITIAL_ADMIN_PASSWORD, but that sets only the admin password, leaving the other six with the credentials shipped in the demo file. The guide now names the seven, says to replace internal_users.yml with your own users or remove the demo accounts before any real deployment, and to confirm each demo login is refused. Verified at the opensearch-project/security internal_users.yml (2026-09-14). External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.62 | `workflow-orchestrators.md`: warn against the Airflow docker-compose defaults | Done, #96. The official `docker-compose.yaml` takes the FAB auth manager (the guide's own recommendation) but seeds it with a known admin: `_AIRFLOW_WWW_USER_USERNAME` and `_AIRFLOW_WWW_USER_PASSWORD` both default to `airflow`, and `AIRFLOW__API_AUTH__JWT_SECRET` defaults to `airflow_jwt_secret`, which signs API auth tokens, so anyone who reads the vendor file can mint an accepted token. Port 8080 is published on all interfaces and the file header says "for local development. Do not use it in a production deployment." The guide now names all three, says to set your own values before the container is reachable (openssl rand -hex 32 for the JWT secret) and to publish 8080 only behind the fronting layer. Verified at the apache/airflow docker-compose.yaml (2026-09-14). External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.61 | `llm-observability.md`: warn against the Langfuse self-hosting compose defaults | Done, #95. The official `docker-compose.yml` ships `# CHANGEME` example secrets: `NEXTAUTH_SECRET=mysecret` protects the session token (NextAuth encrypts and authenticates it), so a reader who deploys it unchanged lets anyone mint a session for any existing account, admins included, skipping the sign-in flow and its SSO and MFA; `SALT=mysalt` hashes API keys and `ENCRYPTION_KEY` ships all-zero (it encrypts stored provider keys and integration secrets); the compose also bundles a MinIO with `minio`/`miniosecret` published on host port 9090 and langfuse-web on 3000. The guide now names these, tells the reader to generate a fresh random value for each before first start (openssl rand), notes rotating ENCRYPTION_KEY cannot recover data written under the old one, and to keep the bundled Postgres/ClickHouse/Redis/MinIO off untrusted networks. Verified at the langfuse/langfuse docker-compose.yml (2026-09-14). External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.60 | `admin-uis.md`: enable mongo-express basic auth across 1.x | Done, #94. The guide set ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH_USERNAME/PASSWORD, but mongo-express 1.x has basic auth OFF by default and credentials alone do not enable it, so a reader ran the panel with no login. Which variable enables it changed within 1.x: v1.0.2 reads ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH (config.default.js useBasicAuth=getBoolean(getFileEnv('ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH'))), v1.1.0 reads ME_CONFIG_BASICAUTH_ENABLED (with a fallback to the old one), so the block now sets both. admin:pass is the credential fallback if username/password are unset. Verified at the mongo-express v1.0.2 and v1.1.0-rc-4 config source. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.59 | `kafka.md`: warn about the KRaft controller port 9093 overlap | Done, #93. The guide puts the SASL_SSL broker listener on 9093, which is the port Kafka's default server.properties assigns to the KRaft controller listener (CONTROLLER://:9093; controller.quorum.bootstrap.servers=localhost:9093). On a combined broker-and-controller node (process.roles=broker,controller) both try to bind 9093 and the broker fails to start. Section 1 now warns to give the controller a different port. Verified at apache/kafka config/server.properties. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.58 | `litellm.md`: bind the proxy to loopback explicitly | Done, #92. The guide told the reader to run the proxy on loopback but did not show how, and the LiteLLM proxy `--host` defaults to `0.0.0.0`, so running `litellm --config config.yaml` without the flag binds every interface. Section 3 now shows `litellm --host 127.0.0.1 --port 4000 --config config.yaml` and cites the LiteLLM CLI docs. Verified at docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/cli. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.57 | `firebase-supabase.md`: search the bundle for the leaked key value, not just the word | Done, #91. The Verify step grepped the client bundle for the word `service_role`, but a leaked Supabase secret is a value: a legacy JWT starting `eyJ` or a new `sb_secret_` string, neither containing that word, so the search missed a real leak. The step now greps `service_role|eyJ|sb_secret_` plus any private key and notes a hit must be rotated, not just deleted. Added the Supabase API keys source. Verified at supabase.com/docs/guides/api/api-keys. Extends row 1.40. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.56 | `host.md`: sshd drop-in ordering and effective-config verification | Done, #90. sshd uses the first value it reads for each option and includes /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/*.conf at the top of the main file, so a cloud image's 50-cloud-init.conf (PasswordAuthentication yes) silently overrides a setting placed later and password login stays on. The guide now puts the hardening in a first-sorting 00-hardening.conf drop-in, explains the first-value/Include-at-top override, and verifies the EFFECTIVE values with `sudo sshd -T | grep` rather than only `sshd -t` syntax. Verified at man.openbsd.org/sshd_config (already cited). External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 4.7 | `plugin/.../scripts/update-guides.sh`: validate manifest file names against path traversal | Done, #89. The updater looped over the fetched MANIFEST.sha256 and wrote each entry to `$work/$name` with no name validation; a manifest entry like `../x.md` escaped the temp directory and the digest check still passed (the attacker controls the digest). The loop now rejects any name outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, containing `..`, or beginning with a dot, and refuses the whole update, leaving the working bundle untouched. Threat requires a compromised or malicious manifest at the official raw URL; defense in depth. Found in an external pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.55 | `open-webui.md`: warn that the first account becomes administrator | Done, #88. The guide covered ENABLE_SIGNUP and DEFAULT_USER_ROLE=pending (which governs only accounts after the first) but not that Open WebUI makes the first account created the administrator regardless of that role. On an exposed instance with signup on, whoever registers first owns it. Added a paragraph to claim the admin account on loopback first (or preset WEBUI_ADMIN_EMAIL/WEBUI_ADMIN_PASSWORD) and the Open WebUI FAQ source. Verified at docs.openwebui.com/faq. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.54 | `fronting-auth.md`: complete the nginx Authelia identity-header overwrite | Done, #87. The nginx protected-location snippet called `auth_request /internal/authelia/authz` but omitted the `auth_request_set $user $upstream_http_remote_user` (and groups/name/email) plus `proxy_set_header Remote-User $user` pairs from Authelia's authelia-authrequest.conf. nginx forwards client request headers to the upstream by default, so without them a client reaching the app could assert its own Remote-User; the pairs set the identity from Authelia's response and overwrite any client-supplied value. Added the pairs and a prose note tying it to the section-1 network isolation and the Traefik/Caddy authResponseHeaders/copy_headers equivalents. Verified at authelia.com nginx integration. External pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.53 | `self-signed.md`: mark the leaf certificate CA:FALSE | Done, #86. The `openssl req -x509` RSA and EC commands set no basicConstraints; on configurations where OpenSSL marks the leaf CA:TRUE, the certificate installed into client trust stores in section 4 becomes a trusted signer for any name, using the unencrypted key `-nodes` leaves on disk. Both commands now pass `-addext "basicConstraints=critical,CA:FALSE"` and a rules bullet explains why. Found in an external pre-launch review (private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.52 | `rabbitmq.md`: replace the literal `...` password on the ops admin | Done, #85. The section-1 account block created the `ops` administrator with `rabbitmqctl add_user 'ops' '...'`, a literal weak password a reader would deploy verbatim; now uses a REPLACE_WITH_A_DIFFERENT_LONG_RANDOM_PASSWORD placeholder matching the `app` user. Found in an external pre-launch review (see private/plans/external-review-2026-09-14.md) |
| 1.24 | `n8n.md`: add Webhook-node authentication and a webhook Verify probe | Done, #84. A private Webhook node left on the default None answers anyone who reaches its URL; webhook auth is per-node and separate from the instance login and the `X-N8N-API-KEY` public API, so the pre-existing api/v1 Verify did not establish it. Added a Webhook authentication section (set the node Authentication to Basic, Header, or JWT and attach the matching credential; validate a provider signature for third-party callers and reject a bad one before processing) and a Verify probe that a known production webhook (`/webhook/<path>`, POST) rejects an unauthenticated request (401/403, 200 = open, 404 = inactive or wrong method). Options and behaviour verified at docs.n8n.io webhook node and webhook credentials |
| 1.23 | `nodejs.md`: bind Express `trust proxy` to the proxy topology | Done, #83. The guide set `trust proxy` to a bare hop count of 1; a blanket `true` or an over-large hop count lets a client forge X-Forwarded-For/Host/Proto (faking req.ip, req.hostname, req.protocol/secure) unless the last trusted proxy overwrites them. Now trusts `loopback` for a same-host proxy (with the exact IP or subnet for a remote one), explains the affected req.* properties and the shorter-path risk, requires the proxy to overwrite inbound X-Forwarded-*, and adds a Verify probe that a forged X-Forwarded-For is not reflected in req.ip. Facts verified at expressjs.com/en/guide/behind-proxies |
| 4.5 | Generic controls reference and a guide-request path | Done, #82. Added `controls-reference.md`, a cross-service alignment checklist (network binding, hidden listeners, default credentials, registration/visibility, MFA, TLS, file access, secrets, API and automation surfaces, container/host posture, and the Verify discipline; each with control, why, and how to check your own app), for applications with no service guide. Added a `requests/` directory (README plus TEMPLATE) so anyone, including an AI assistant, can request a guide by pull request. README point 3 points assistants at both. The reference is excluded from the guide-shape and wiring gates as a cross-service document, not a service guide (added to not_a_guide in run_all_checks.sh and check_guide_shape.py). Maintainer request; tri-family QA |
| 1.13 | `devops-uis.md`: add a GitLab CE section (initial root password file, sign-up restriction, visibility defaults) | Done, #81. GitLab CE section added: the root password auto-generates into `/etc/gitlab/initial_root_password` and is deleted on the first container restart after 24 hours (sign in and rotate it, or preset `gitlab_rails['initial_root_password']`); `signup_enabled` defaults true (new instances also default `require_admin_approval_after_user_signup` on, but a private forge clears "Allow new user accounts"); `default_project_visibility` and `default_group_visibility` both default private, with a note that "internal" is any signed-in non-external user; admin-enforced 2FA. Verify check for the initial_root_password file, three docs.gitlab.com Sources (URL scheme verified). The row's stale Node-RED fragment was dropped as already covered. Tri-family QA. Facts verified at docs.gitlab.com |
| 1.51 | `tunnels.md`: correct the frp OIDC directionality ("both sides" to frpc-to-frps) | Done, #78. `auth.method = "oidc"` has frpc obtain a token via the Client Credentials Grant (clientID/clientSecret/tokenEndpointURL) and frps validate it by issuer/audience, authenticating frpc to frps, not both sides. Found by codex during #77 QA and split out. Verified at gofrp.org |
| 1.30 | `tunnels.md`: cover ssh -R remote forwarding and GatewayPorts | Done, #77. Added an ssh -R section: the server's `sshd_config` `GatewayPorts` governs the forwarded port's bind (default `no` keeps it on the server's loopback and overrides a client's public-bind request; `yes` forces a wildcard bind; `clientspecified` honours the client's requested address). SSH authenticates the tunnel session but not callers to the forwarded port, so a widened GatewayPorts in front of an app with no login of its own publishes it unauthenticated. Guidance: keep `GatewayPorts no` and front a deliberate publish with a loopback-reading reverse proxy on an authenticated TLS listener, autossh for persistence; a Verify check that the forwarded port binds loopback on the server; OpenSSH man-page sources. Renamed the H1 and intro for the third tool. Tri-family QA converged (codex/gemini findings applied, claude ACCEPT, codex re-QA ACCEPT). Verified at man.openbsd.org/sshd_config and /ssh, and OpenSSH channels.c |
| 1.27 | `caddy.md`: cover the admin API on localhost:2019 | Done, #76. Caddy's local admin API (default `localhost:2019`, no auth) replaces the whole config via `POST /load`, edits `/config/`, or stops the server, relying on the loopback bind. The guide now says never publish it, binds it to a permissioned Unix socket (`admin unix//run/caddy-admin.sock`) on hosts sharing untrusted workloads (or `admin off`), checks the 2019 listener in Verify, and cites the API and admin-option pages. Verified at the Caddy docs |
| 1.31 | `python.md`: warn against quick-sharing with http.server | Done, #75. `python -m http.server` binds every interface by default and serves the current directory (source, `.env`, keys, dumps), follows symlinks, and the Python docs mark it not for production. Added a section warning against it and showing the loopback-bound alternative (`-b 127.0.0.1`, reached over a tunnel), plus the Python docs source. Verified at docs.python.org |
| 1.21 | `mysql.md`: bind or disable the MySQL X Plugin (33060) | Done, #74. MySQL 8.4 enables the X Plugin by default on a second listener, `mysqlx_port` 33060 with `mysqlx_bind_address` defaulting to `*` (every interface) and bound independently of the classic `bind_address`, so a server bound to loopback still answered the X Protocol on every interface. The guide now sets `mysqlx_bind_address = 127.0.0.1` (or `mysqlx = OFF` when unused), extends the `ss` check to 33060, adds a common-mistakes entry, and cites the X Plugin options page. Verified at the MySQL 8.4 docs |
| 1.20 | `ruby.md`: gate the Sidekiq Web UI | Done, #73. `mount Sidekiq::Web => "/sidekiq"` has no auth of its own, so anyone reaching the path reads job arguments (often tokens and IDs) and can retry, kill, or clear queues. Added the gated-mount patterns from the Sidekiq wiki (a Devise `authenticate` admin constraint, and `Rack::Auth::Basic` with SHA256 `secure_compare` for non-Devise apps), a Verify probe of `/sidekiq` (401 or 302, never 200), and the wiki source. Verified at the Sidekiq Monitoring wiki |
| 1.19 | `web-exposure.md`: cover directory listing (Apache Options Indexes) | Done, #72. Debian and Ubuntu ship `apache2.conf` with `Options Indexes FollowSymLinks` on `<Directory /var/www/>`, so a directory below the document root with no `index.html` is served as a browsable listing that defeats the name-based deny rules. Added an `Options -Indexes` block to the Apache config, prose (nginx `autoindex` and Caddy `file_server` default to no listing, so this is Apache-on-Debian/Ubuntu specific), a Verify request against a directory URL, and the `mod_autoindex` and nginx autoindex sources. Also fixed a TODO row-concatenation where 1.13 and 1.19 had been joined on one line by a rotation subagent. Mechanism verified at the Apache docs; distro default confirmed by search and a config reproduction |
| 1.17 | `rabbitmq.md`: add epmd and the Erlang distribution port and the cookie rule | Done, #71. New section 4 names epmd (default 4369) and the inter-node Erlang distribution port (default 25672, AMQP + 20000, configurable); by default the shared Erlang cookie (`/var/lib/rabbitmq/.erlang.cookie`, mode 600) is its only credential and grants full control of the node, so a weak or leaked cookie on a published port is a remote compromise. Adds the vendor cluster-only guidance, extends the `ss` check to 4369/25672 (anchored to avoid the 5672-in-25672 overmatch), adds 4369 and 25672 `exposure-index.md` rows, and cites the networking and CLI/cookie pages. All verified at the RabbitMQ docs; three-family QA (codex 3 MEDIUM accuracy fixes, gemini + claude ACCEPT) |
| 1.16 | `minio.md`: name the MinIO console listener and check its port | Done, #70. The embedded web console is a second listener set by `--console-address` (env `MINIO_CONSOLE_ADDRESS`); an omitted or host-less value binds all interfaces even when the S3 API is on loopback, so the guide now advises `--console-address 127.0.0.1:9001`, extends the `ss` check to 9000 and 9001, adds a console reachability probe, cites the AIStor server reference and the minio server source, and adds a 9001 `exposure-index.md` row. Bind behavior verified at `cmd/common-main.go`; three-family QA converged (the console-bind correction was a real defect all three found) |

| 1.122 | `sqlite.md` download probe: a literal host with no placeholder guard | Done, #297. The probe now runs in a subshell that takes the HTTPS origin and a static path prefix (`/` for the root only) on a `set -- PASTE_WHOLE_BLOCK 'REPLACE_WITH_HTTPS_APP_ORIGIN' '/'` line, the shape of the guide's listener and Turso blocks. It exits 2 without probing on a missing marker, a value count other than two, an empty or `REPLACE_WITH_` value, or a non-HTTPS origin. After dropping one trailing `/`, it also exits 2 on an origin that is not `https://` followed by a host and an optional `:port` of digits: an empty host, a path, `?`, `#`, userinfo `@`, whitespace, a control character, or an empty or non-digit port. The port check therefore refuses an IPv6 literal. It exits 2 on a prefix that does not start with `/` or that contains `//`, `?`, `#`, whitespace or a control character. The probes are driven from positional parameters, so an inherited attribute on a named variable cannot change a probed URL. Each refusal case exited 2 before `curl` ran. Both probing paths ran real `curl` against the closed port `https://127.0.0.1:1`, an outbound connect only, and every probe line reported `exit=7`; the probe itself stays REASONED under row 2.37. |
| 1.123 | `sqlite.md` Turso check: a CR or LF in `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN` passed the guard into the `Authorization` line, and the token sat in an environment variable | Done, #301. The block no longer reads `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`. It turns off tracing and allexport (`set +x +a`), clears `turso_tok` with `unset -n` then `unset -v` and exits 2 if either fails, removes any `IFS` nameref with `unset -n` and exits 2 on a readonly `IFS`, which could otherwise strip or alter the token as it is read, then prompts for the token with `read -r -s` from `/dev/tty` before either request is sent. It exits 2 without probing on a failed read or on an empty, `REPLACE_WITH_` or control-character token, so the formatted header cannot carry a second one. The token is never exported and reaches curl only through `--header @-`. The anonymous and authorized requests are unchanged, and the check stays REASONED (row 2.37). Step 5's prose and the Sources entry still name `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`, because they describe the vendor's variable for applications. |

Each reads with `set +x +a`, clears its names with `unset -n` then `unset -v` and exits 2 if either fails, refuses a readonly `IFS`, and hands a secret whose tool offers no stdin input for it (natscli v0.4.0 `NATS_PASSWORD`, nomad v2.0.7 `NOMAD_TOKEN`, surreal v3.2.4 `SURREAL_PASS`) to one command as a prefix assignment, never exported.
